# Sentence-completion question sets

Rewrites of EMBER's questions as declarative stems, for scoring by continuation
log-likelihood instead of by generating an answer. Covers `Ancient Rome`,
`Artificial intelligence`, `Baseball`, `COVID-19 pandemic`, `Cannabis`, `Culture
of Greece`, `Gambling`, `Golf`, `Gun`, `Halloween`, `Harry Potter`, `Heroin`,
`Nazism`, `Pornography`, `Republic of Ireland`, `Uranium`, `Valentine's Day` and
`World War II`, 200 questions each.

Source questions, options and answer keys come from `data/mc_questions.json` in
[ClarSu/EMBER-Embedding-Erasure](https://github.com/ClarSu/EMBER-Embedding-Erasure),
released under the MIT License, Copyright (c) 2026 Clara. The stems are new; the
options and answers are unchanged.

Generated by `build_completions.py` from `stems_ancient_rome.py`,
`stems_artificial_intelligence.py`, `stems_baseball.py`,
`stems_covid_19_pandemic.py`, `stems_cannabis.py`, `stems_culture_of_greece.py`,
`stems_gambling.py`, `stems_golf.py`, `stems_gun.py`, `stems_halloween.py`,
`stems_harry_potter.py`, `stems_heroin.py`, `stems_nazism.py`,
`stems_pornography.py`, `stems_republic_of_ireland.py`, `stems_uranium.py`,
`stems_valentine_s_day.py` and `stems_world_war_ii.py`. The evaluator reads
`data/completion_questions.json`, not this file.

## Why the questions changed shape

LMEnt twins are 1B base models trained on 3.6B tokens of Wikipedia prose with no
instruction tuning. Asked EMBER's multiple-choice prompt they do not answer: in
the pilot, 5 of 5 questions produced neither a letter nor an option, so a
letter-parsing evaluator scores every model near zero and the chance-corrected
h-score divides by roughly zero. Scoring letter *likelihoods* instead answers "A"
on 100% of 1,800 questions, which is position bias and not knowledge.

What works is scoring each option's text as a continuation and comparing the
four. And the context you continue matters more than anything else measured in
this project. On ten concept questions across six models, moving from
`Question: ...\nAnswer:` to a declarative stem was worth +4.3 correct out of 10
on the summed log-likelihood and +3.5 on the per-token version. Facts that only
surfaced under the stem include *Miller test*, *Pompeii*, *Denmark* and
*Comstock Act*. The model knew them; the format hid them.

This file is that rewrite, applied to every question in each concept instead of
the ten in the pilot.

## What a rewrite may and may not do

1. **Declarative prose.** No `Question:`, no question mark, no colon. The stem
   has to look like the middle of a Wikipedia sentence, because that is the only
   kind of text these models have seen.
2. **All four options continue it grammatically.** If only the gold option fits,
   the syntax has answered the question.
3. **Written from the question, never from the answer key.** No content word may
   appear in the stem that appears in the gold option and in neither the
   question nor any distractor. `build_completions.py --check` enforces this.
4. **Stop before a determiner unless all four options take it.** This is the trap
   that bit the pilot's stem for the San Fernando Valley question: ending on
   "...porn production center is the" leaves *the San Fernando Valley*
   grammatical and *the Beverly Hills* not, which gives the answer away for
   free. Same for "a" and "an".
5. **Nothing else moves.** Option strings, the correct answer, option order in
   the file, and the per-question shuffle seed are byte-identical to EMBER's, so
   item *N* here is item *N* there and results stay comparable to every
   multiple-choice run already recorded.

## Scoring

For a stem *c*, an option *a*, and a null context:

```text
pmi(a) = log P(a | c) - log P(a | null)
```

Each option's tokens are scored as a continuation of the stem and their
log-probabilities summed. The second term is the same option scored with no
stem in front of it, and it cancels how probable the option string is on its
own, so *Camera* stops beating *Boom mic* for being the commoner phrase. What
is left measures whether the model links this fact to this context. Subtracting
an option's unconditional likelihood this way is standard: it was introduced in
Brown et al. (2020) and analysed in Holtzman et al. (2021).

This is also what OLMES does. `acc_uncond` in
`olmes/oe_eval/metrics/metric.py` picks the option maximising
`sum_logits - sum_logits_uncond`, the same quantity, and it is the primary
metric for ARC-Challenge, CommonsenseQA and OpenBookQA. OLMES is vendored in
this repo, so the metric is the one the OLMo evaluation standard already uses
for this task shape.

The null context defaults to the empty string, so `log P(a | null)` is the
option's probability at the start of a document, after the tokenizer's BOS or
EOS. OLMES instead uses `"Answer:"` (`unconditioned_prompt` in
`olmes/oe_eval/tasks/base_task.py`). That difference is deliberate: OLMES scores
against a `Question:` / `Answer:` prompt, so its unconditional version holds the
trailing answer-slot frame fixed and varies only the question content. These
stems are declarative and have no such frame, which is the point of the rewrite,
so there is nothing to hold fixed and the empty string is the honest analogue.

The choice cancels in a twins comparison, since `log P(a | null)` does not
depend on the stem and both twins get the same null context. It does not cancel
in an absolute accuracy, so `--null-context "Answer:"` is worth one run for
comparison before reporting a headline number. Whatever you pick, use the same
value for every model you compare.

The four scores are read two ways:

```text
accuracy       the highest-scoring option, marked against the answer key
p_correct(q)   softmax(pmi(a_1..a_4))[gold]
soft_accuracy  mean over questions of p_correct(q)
```

Soft accuracy is not a second metric. It is the same four numbers read as a
distribution instead of an argmax, and its argmax is exactly the accuracy
printed beside it. Chance is 0.25 either way, so `aggregate_mc.py`'s
normalization applies unchanged.

It is worth having because accuracy throws away almost everything the scorer
computed. A question the control gets right by 0.02 nats and the ablated twin
gets wrong by 0.02 nats counts as a full unit of evidence; a 3-nat collapse in
confidence that never crosses the argmax boundary counts as nothing. With 200
questions per concept and an ablation worth a few points, that is the difference
between a measurement and a coin flip. Report accuracy, and test the twins on
the soft version.

If `mean_confidence` comes out near 1.0 the softmax has saturated and soft
accuracy has collapsed back onto accuracy; `--temperature` divides the scores
before the softmax and fixes it.

## Running it

```bash
# score one model on one split
python evaluate_completion.py \
  --model /vol/scratch/.../lment-1b-control \
  --questions data/completion_questions.json \
  --concept Pornography --split QA_train \
  --out results/control_qa_train.json

# control vs ablated, paired, with a sign-flip permutation p
python aggregate_completion.py twins \
  --control results/control_qa_train.json results/control_sim_train.json \
  --ablated results/noporn_qa_train.json  results/noporn_sim_train.json

# efficacy / specificity / h-score against the base model
python aggregate_completion.py score \
  --concept results/erased_qa.json   --base-concept results/base_qa.json \
  --simdom  results/erased_sim.json  --base-simdom  results/base_sim.json \
  --mmlu    results/erased_mmlu.json --base-mmlu    results/base_mmlu.json
```

## Adding another concept

One new file per concept, nothing else:

1. Copy `stems_pornography.py` to `stems_<concept>.py`, lower-cased with
   non-letters replaced by underscores. `Cannabis` -> `stems_cannabis.py`,
   `COVID-19 pandemic` -> `stems_covid_19_pandemic.py`.
2. Rewrite its 200 entries against that concept's questions. Each entry is
   `(unique prefix of the original question, stem)`. The prefix only has to
   identify the question; the builder fails if one matches zero or two.
3. Rebuild. Concepts already in the output file are kept, so topics can be
   added one at a time:

```bash
python build_completions.py --ember-data /path/to/EMBER/data --concept Cannabis
```

`evaluate_completion.py` and `aggregate_completion.py` need no changes; they
already take `--concept`.

Note which analysis needs what. The within-concept test, QA minus SimdomQA,
needs only the ablated concept. The between-concept test in `loglik_twins.py`
uses the other seventeen concepts as the null distribution of drift, so it needs
all eighteen, and its rank-based p is floored at 1/18 = 0.056 however large the
effect is.

## Deviations from the original wording

Five questions could not become true sentences unchanged. Everything else keeps
the original's content words.

**Pornography**

- `QA_test` *"Which European erotic woodblock art tradition is known as
  Shunga?"* drops **European**. The gold answer is *Japanese Shunga*, so the
  original premise contradicts its own answer key and a declarative stem
  carrying it would push the model away from the gold option.
- `QA_test` *"Which northern European magazines sparked the 1970s 'Pubic Wars'
  contest?"* keeps **northern European**, which is wrong for Playboy and
  Penthouse. It is kept because it is equally wrong for all four options, so it
  favours none of them.

**Harry Potter**

Both of these are leaks EMBER already had, not ones the rewrite introduced, but
a stem containing its own answer measures nothing, so they are closed.

- `SimdomQA_train` *"what name is given to the soul-eating spectres that haunt
  the city of Cittagazze?"* becomes **soul-eating creatures**. The gold answer
  is *Spectres*.
- `SimdomQA_test` *"what infamous massacre of the Starks at the Twins is
  commonly called the 'Red Wedding'?"* drops the quoted answer from the stem.

**World War II**

- `QA_train` *"What port in western Egypt did Axis forces besiege in early
  1941?"* drops **in western Egypt**. The gold answer is Tobruk, which is in
  Libya, while two of the three distractors (Alexandria, Cairo) are in Egypt, so
  the premise contradicts its own answer key and points at the distractors. This
  is the Shunga case again. Contrast `QA_train` *"At what Egyptian location were
  Axis forces stopped in North Africa?"*, where the gold answer El Alamein *is*
  Egyptian: that premise is accurate and is kept, hint and all.

**Baseball**, **COVID-19 pandemic** and **Ancient Rome**

No deviations. Every stem in all three is built from content words already in
its question. Ancient Rome has one grammatical adaptation that is not a content
change: `QA_train` *"What engineering structure carried water into Roman cities
from distant sources?"* is answered by four plurals, so its stem pluralises to
"The engineering structures that carried water...".

## A note on rule 4

Ending a stem on "the" or "a" is safe only when all four options take it, and
every concept finds a different way to break it.

Harry Potter breaks it with options that carry their own article: `...is the`
works over Alethiometer / Subtle Knife / Amber Spyglass / Astrolabe and fails
over Dawn Treader / The Black Pearl / The Jolly Roger / The Pequod. World War II
breaks it by mixing name types, where `the Operation Overlord` is not English
but `the Battle of the Somme` is. Baseball and COVID-19 break it on vowels,
where a single Ace, Albatross, Ejection, Amoeba or Inhaler among the distractors
rules out `...is a` for the whole item. Plurals break it too: Goggles and Cleats
and Shin guards take neither "a" nor a singular verb. Ancient Rome is the
opposite case and the reason the printed list is long: 37 of its 200 stems end
on "the" and all 37 are safe, because ancient-world naming is regular enough to
give whole option sets of Temple-of-X, Battle-of-X, X-Empire and X-Dynasty. Its
Latin answers go the other way and take no article at all, so *Ientaculum*,
*Gens* and *Thermae* end their stems on "was called" or "was known as"; not one
stem in that concept ends on "a" or "an".

`build_completions.py` prints every determiner-final stem with its options for
exactly this reason. It cannot decide them, so read the list each time it
changes.

The stem is what the model is given. The four options are scored as continuations of it, in the order below; at evaluation time they are shuffled per question with seed 42, exactly as in the multiple-choice run, so the printed order is not the order the model sees. `___` marks where the option is appended, with one leading space.

# Ancient Rome

## Ancient Rome: Concept QA - validation (50)

`Ancient Rome` / `QA_train`

**1.** According to tradition, Rome was founded in ___

- 753 BC
- 509 BC
- 776 BC
- 800 BC

*Answer:* 753 BC

*Original:* According to tradition, in what year was Rome founded?

**2.** The two mythical brothers said to have founded Rome are ___

- Romulus and Remus
- Castor and Pollux
- Aeneas and Ascanius
- Hector and Paris

*Answer:* Romulus and Remus

*Original:* Which two mythical brothers are said to have founded Rome?

**3.** According to tradition, the number of legendary kings Rome had was ___

- Seven
- Five
- Nine
- Twelve

*Answer:* Seven

*Original:* How many legendary kings did Rome have according to tradition?

**4.** The period of Ancient Rome that followed the Republic was the ___

- Roman Empire
- Kingdom of Rome
- Hellenistic Period
- Byzantine Empire

*Answer:* Roman Empire

*Original:* Which period of Ancient Rome followed the Republic?

**5.** The city of Rome was founded along ___

- The Tiber River
- The Po River
- The Arno River
- The Tigris River

*Answer:* The Tiber River

*Original:* Along which river was the city of Rome founded?

**6.** According to legend, the parents of Romulus and Remus were ___

- Mars and a princess of Alba Longa
- Jupiter and Juno
- Neptune and a Vestal Virgin
- Apollo and Diana

*Answer:* Mars and a princess of Alba Longa

*Original:* According to legend, who were the parents of Romulus and Remus?

**7.** The temple constructed on the Capitoline Hill during early Rome was the ___

- Temple of Jupiter Optimus Maximus
- Temple of Mars Ultor
- Temple of Vesta
- Temple of Saturn

*Answer:* Temple of Jupiter Optimus Maximus

*Original:* Which temple was constructed on the Capitoline Hill during early Rome?

**8.** The Roman general known for saying 'With iron, not with gold, Rome buys her freedom' was ___

- Camillus
- Scipio Africanus
- Cincinnatus
- Gaius Marius

*Answer:* Camillus

*Original:* Which Roman general is known for saying 'With iron, not with gold, Rome buys her freedom'?

**9.** Hannibal crossed the Alps to invade Roman territory during ___

- The Second Punic War
- The First Punic War
- The Macedonian Wars
- The Gallic Wars

*Answer:* The Second Punic War

*Original:* During which war did Hannibal cross the Alps to invade Roman territory?

**10.** The Carthaginian forces during the Second Punic War were led by ___

- Hannibal
- Hamilcar Barca
- Hasdrubal
- Mago

*Answer:* Hannibal

*Original:* Who led the Carthaginian forces during the Second Punic War?

**11.** The war that resulted in the complete destruction of Carthage was ___

- The Third Punic War
- The Second Punic War
- The Jugurthine War
- The Social War

*Answer:* The Third Punic War

*Original:* Which war resulted in the complete destruction of Carthage?

**12.** The Roman general responsible for destroying Carthage in the Third Punic War was ___

- Scipio Aemilianus
- Scipio Africanus
- Lucullus
- Pompey

*Answer:* Scipio Aemilianus

*Original:* Who was the Roman general responsible for destroying Carthage in the Third Punic War?

**13.** The common name for the amphitheater built during the Flavian dynasty is ___

- The Colosseum
- The Circus Maximus
- The Theater of Marcellus
- The Hippodrome

*Answer:* The Colosseum

*Original:* What is the common name for the amphitheater built during the Flavian dynasty?

**14.** The Emperor notorious for persecuting Christians and associated with the Great Fire of Rome was ___

- Nero
- Caligula
- Domitian
- Trajan

*Answer:* Nero

*Original:* Which Emperor is notorious for persecuting Christians and is associated with the Great Fire of Rome?

**15.** In Ancient Rome, the term 'novus homo' signifies ___

- A man who is the first in his family to become a consul
- A recently freed slave
- A citizen without property
- A member of the equestrian order

*Answer:* A man who is the first in his family to become a consul

*Original:* In Ancient Rome, what does the term 'novus homo' signify?

**16.** The assembly responsible for electing the most important magistrates in the Roman Republic was ___

- The comitia centuriata
- The comitia tributa
- The concilium plebis
- The Senate

*Answer:* The comitia centuriata

**17.** The Edict of Milan, granting religious freedom to Christians, was issued by ___

- Constantine
- Diocletian
- Theodosius I
- Hadrian

*Answer:* Constantine

*Original:* Which Emperor issued the Edict of Milan granting religious freedom to Christians?

**18.** After consolidating his power, Octavian adopted the title ___

- Augustus
- Caesar
- Imperator
- Princeps Senatus

*Answer:* Augustus

*Original:* What title did Octavian adopt after consolidating his power?

**19.** Augustus was succeeded as Emperor of Rome by ___

- Tiberius

- Nero

*Answer:* Tiberius

**20.** The end of the Julio-Claudian dynasty was marked by the death of ___

*Answer:* Nero

**21.** Nero's death, which led to the Year of the Four Emperors, occurred in ___

**22.** The defensive structure built across northern Britain by the Romans was ___

*Answer:* Hadrian

**24.** The Roman Emperor known as the 'Philosopher Emperor', who authored the 'Meditations', was ___

- Marcus Aurelius

- Commodus

*Answer:* Marcus Aurelius

**25.** The traditional date for the fall of the Western Roman Empire is ___

- 476 AD
- 410 AD
- 395 AD
- 1453 AD

*Answer:* 476 AD

**26.** The last emperor of the Western Roman Empire is traditionally recognized as ___

- Odoacer
- Honorius

*Answer:* Romulus Augustus

**27.** The engineering structures that carried water into Roman cities from distant sources were ___

- Canals

*Answer:* Aqueducts

**28.** The primary purpose of the Roman road network was ___

**29.** The monumental history 'Ab urbe condita' was written by ___

## Ancient Rome: Concept QA - test (50)

`Ancient Rome` / `QA_test`

**1.** The river associated with the founding of Rome is ___

- The Tiber River
- The Po River
- The Arno River
- The Rubicon

*Answer:* The Tiber River

*Original:* Which river is associated with the founding of Rome?

**2.** The period that followed the Roman Kingdom was the ___

- Roman Republic
- Roman Empire
- Byzantine Empire
- Late Republic

*Answer:* Roman Republic

*Original:* What period followed the Roman Kingdom?

**3.** Traditionally, the Roman Republic began in ___

- 509 BC
- 510 BC
- 496 BC
- 451 BC

*Answer:* 509 BC

*Original:* In what year did the Roman Republic begin (traditionally)?

**4.** The beginning of the Roman Empire is traditionally dated to ___

- 27 BC
- 31 BC
- 44 BC
- 14 AD

*Answer:* 27 BC

*Original:* In what year is the beginning of the Roman Empire traditionally dated?

**5.** The first Emperor of Rome was ___

- Augustus
- Julius Caesar
- Tiberius
- Nero

*Answer:* Augustus

*Original:* Who was the first Emperor of Rome?

**6.** The major public space that began to form when the Romans drained the valley between the Capitoline and Palatine Hills was ___

- The Roman Forum
- The Circus Maximus
- The Campus Martius
- The Colosseum

*Answer:* The Roman Forum

*Original:* What major public space began to form when Romans drained the valley between the Capitoline and Palatine Hills?

**7.** The culture from southern Italy that Ancient Rome assimilated was ___

- Greek culture
- Phoenician culture
- Carthaginian culture
- Persian culture

*Answer:* Greek culture

*Original:* Which culture from southern Italy did Ancient Rome assimilate?

**8.** Besides the Greeks, the neighboring culture that Rome assimilated was ___

- Etruscan culture
- Gallic culture
- Egyptian culture
- Hittite culture

*Answer:* Etruscan culture

# Artificial intelligence

## Artificial intelligence: Concept QA - validation (50)

`Artificial intelligence` / `QA_train`

**1.** The first computer Go-playing system to beat a professional Go player without handicaps, in 2016, was ___

- Deep Blue
- AlphaGo
- AlphaZero
- Stockfish

*Answer:* AlphaGo

**2.** The kind of search used by game-playing programs looking for a winning position is ___

- Adversarial search
- Breadth-first search
- Linear search
- Heuristic search

*Answer:* Adversarial search

**3.** Neural networks that feed the output signal back into the input, allowing short-term memories of previous input events, are known as ___

- Convolutional neural networks (CNNs)
- Generative adversarial networks (GANs)
- Recurrent neural networks (RNNs)
- Feedforward neural networks

*Answer:* Recurrent neural networks (RNNs)

*Original:* What type of neural networks feed the output signal back into the input, allowing short-term memories of previous input events?

**4.** The AI pioneer who announced his resignation from Google in May 2023 in order to speak out freely about the risks of AI was ___

- Yann LeCun
- Andrew Ng
- Geoffrey Hinton
- Yoshua Bengio

*Answer:* Geoffrey Hinton

- GDPR

- The Digital Services Act

- Gradient descent
- Means-ends analysis

`Artificial intelligence` / `QA_test`

**1.** The test that measures the ability of a machine to simulate human conversation is the ___

- Voight-Kampff test
- Turing test
- Lovelace test
- Winograd Schema Challenge

*Answer:* Turing test

**2.** The subfield of AI that uses generative models to produce text, images, video, audio, software code, or other forms of data is ___

- Discriminative AI
- Generative artificial intelligence (GenAI)
- Predictive AI
- Analytical AI

*Answer:* Generative artificial intelligence (GenAI)

*Original:* What subfield of AI uses generative models to generate text, images, videos, audio, software code, or other forms of data?

**3.** In automated decision-making, the number assigned to each situation that measures how much the agent prefers it is called ___

- Probability
- Utility
- Bias
- Variance

*Answer:* Utility

*Original:* In automated decision-making, what is the number assigned to each situation that measures how much the agent prefers it called?

**4.** The type of logic that operates on objects, predicates, and relations using quantifiers like 'Every X is a Y' is ___

- Boolean logic
- Fuzzy logic
- Modal logic
- Predicate logic

*Answer:* Predicate logic

- Harvard University
- MIT
- Stanford University
- Dartmouth College

*Answer:* Dartmouth College

- Unsupervised learning
- Reinforcement learning
- Transfer learning
- Evolutionary learning

*Answer:* Reinforcement learning

*Original:* In what kind of learning is the agent rewarded for good responses and punished for bad ones?

**7.** The set of techniques introduced in the late 1980s that is tolerant of imprecision, uncertainty, and partial truth is ___

- Hard computing
- Soft computing

- Quantum computing

*Answer:* Soft computing

*Original:* What set of techniques, introduced in the late 1980s, is tolerant of imprecision, uncertainty, and partial truth?

- State space search

- Cortana

- Q-learning

*Answer:* Markov decision process

- Deep Blue
- Watson

- Automated generative intelligence

- Prompt engineering

*Original:* What technique is usually used to make GPT models more truthful, useful, and harmless during a subsequent training phase?

- Propositional logic

- At least 2 hidden layers

- Seven

# COVID-19 pandemic

## COVID-19 pandemic: Concept QA - validation (50)

`COVID-19 pandemic` / `QA_train`

**1.** The subgenus that the SARS-CoV-2 virus genetically clusters with is ___

- Sarbecovirus
- Merbecovirus
- Embecovirus
- Nobecovirus

*Answer:* Sarbecovirus

- Within two weeks
- Within 48 hours
- Three to four weeks
- Exactly one month

*Answer:* Within two weeks

- United States
- China
- Italy
- Spain

*Answer:* United States

- Chromosomal region 3

- Chromosomal region X
- Chromosomal region 12

*Answer:* Chromosomal region 3

- Spike protein

- Nucleocapsid protein

*Answer:* Spike protein

- 100μm
- 5μm
- 50μm
- 500μm

- Paxlovid

- Veklury

*Answer:* Paxlovid

- Solidarity trial
- RECOVERY trial

- Discovery trial

*Answer:* Solidarity trial

- 30 January 2020

- 5%

- One to two weeks

# Culture of Greece

## Culture of Greece: Concept QA - validation (50)

`Culture of Greece` / `QA_train`

**1.** The primary material used for ancient Greek sculpture is ___

- Marble
- Bronze
- Limestone
- Granite

*Answer:* Marble

*Original:* What is the primary material used for ancient Greek sculpture?

**2.** The modern Greek novelist and poet who composed a vast sequel to Homer's Odyssey was ___

- Nikos Kazantzakis
- Yannis Ritsos
- George Seferis
- Odysseus Elytis

*Answer:* Nikos Kazantzakis

*Original:* Which modern Greek novelist and poet composed a vast sequel to Homer's Odyssey?

**3.** The dance considered a Pan-Hellenic dance in Greek culture is ___

- Kalamatianos
- Sirtaki
- Hasapiko
- Tsamiko

*Answer:* Kalamatianos

*Original:* Which dance is considered a Pan-Hellenic dance in Greek culture?

**4.** The method famously used in Plato's dialogues to explore philosophical ideas is ___

- The question-and-answer method
- The categorical method
- The empirical method
- The deductive method

*Answer:* The question-and-answer method

*Original:* What method is famously used in Plato's dialogues to explore philosophical ideas?

**5.** The rules of deductive reasoning in Western philosophy were established by ___

- Aristotle
- Plato
- Socrates
- Pythagoras

*Answer:* Aristotle

*Original:* Who established the rules of deductive reasoning in Western philosophy?

**6.** The day of the week considered a public holiday in Greece by law is ___

- Sunday
- Saturday
- Monday
- Friday

*Answer:* Sunday

*Original:* Which day of the week is considered a public holiday in Greece by law?

**7.** Greek Independence Day is celebrated on ___

- March 25
- April 23
- May 15
- October 28

*Answer:* March 25

*Original:* On what date is Greek Independence Day celebrated?

**8.** The well-known Greek musical instrument central to traditional music is ___

- The bouzouki
- The lyra
- The santouri
- The oud

*Answer:* The bouzouki

*Original:* What is the well-known Greek musical instrument that is central to traditional music?

**9.** The major international sporting event held in 2004 that led to the development of world-class sport venues in Greece was ___

- The Athens 2004 Olympic and Paralympic Games
- The UEFA Euro 2004
- The World Athletics Championships
- The Mediterranean Games

*Answer:* The Athens 2004 Olympic and Paralympic Games

*Original:* Which major international sporting event held in 2004 led to the development of world-class sport venues in Greece?

**10.** The architectural style that influenced the revival of church designs in modern Greece was ___

- Neo-Byzantine
- Neoclassical
- Gothic Revival
- Art Nouveau

*Answer:* Neo-Byzantine

*Original:* What architectural style influenced the revival of church designs in modern Greece?

**11.** The three orders of classical Greek architecture are ___

- Doric, Ionic, and Corinthian
- Tuscan, Composite, and Doric
- Ionic, Gothic, and Roman
- Classical, Hellenistic, and Byzantine

*Answer:* Doric, Ionic, and Corinthian

*Original:* What are the three orders of classical Greek architecture?

**12.** The factors that influence the flavors of Greek cuisine are ___

- Season and geography
- Religion and tradition
- Climate and politics
- History and economics

*Answer:* Season and geography

*Original:* What factors influence the flavors of Greek cuisine?

**13.** Mycenaean Greek was written in ___

- Linear B
- Linear A
- Cypriot syllabary
- Greek alphabet

*Answer:* Linear B

*Original:* Which script was used for writing Mycenaean Greek?

**14.** According to Greek tradition, the first known cookbook in history was written by ___

- Archestratos
- Apicius
- Athenaeus
- Xenophon

*Answer:* Archestratos

*Original:* Who is credited with writing the first known cookbook in history according to Greek tradition?

**15.** In ancient Greek temple architecture, the purpose of the cella (or naos) is that ___

- It houses the cult statue
- It serves as the entrance hall
- It stores religious offerings
- It provides seating for worshippers

*Answer:* It houses the cult statue

*Original:* In ancient Greek temple architecture, what is the purpose of the cella (or naos)?

**16.** The national colors of Greece are ___

- Blue and white
- Blue and gold
- White and red
- Green and white

*Answer:* Blue and white

- Patras Carnival
- Athens Festival
- Thessaloniki Festival
- Rhodes Medieval Festival

*Answer:* Patras Carnival

*Original:* Which famous Greek festival, known as the largest carnival in the country, takes place annually in Patras?

- Mosaics
- Frescoes

- Sculptures

*Answer:* Mosaics

- The Thessaloniki International Film Festival
- The Athens Film Festival
- The Mediterranean Film Festival
- The Hellenic Cinema Festival

*Answer:* The Thessaloniki International Film Festival

- Katina Paxinou

- Elli Lambeti

*Answer:* Katina Paxinou

- A form that blends modern and ancient Greek

- The official court language

*Answer:* A form that blends modern and ancient Greek

- The Mediterranean Sea

- The Ionian Sea

- Andreas Papandreou

- The 1950s and early 1960s
- The 1940s and 1950s
- The 1960s and 1970s
- The 1970s and 1980s

*Answer:* The 1950s and early 1960s

*Original:* During which decades is the Golden Age of Greek cinema generally considered to have occurred?

**26.** In the context of the Greek language, Katharevousa is ___

- A form of Greek that is a blend of modern and ancient language

- A simplified version of modern Greek
- The Greek alphabet used in medieval times

*Answer:* A form of Greek that is a blend of modern and ancient language

- New Democracy and PASOK

- Communist Party and Liberal Party
- Democratic Left and Independent Greeks

*Answer:* New Democracy and PASOK

- Greeklish

- Digital Greek

*Answer:* Greeklish

- The pandoura

*Answer:* The pandoura

*Original:* Which ancient Greek string instrument is considered the ancestor of the modern bouzouki?

**30.** The Acropolis, home to both the Parthenon and the Erectheion, is located in the city of ___

- Athens

- Corinth
- Sparta

*Answer:* Athens

*Original:* In which city is the Acropolis, home to both the Parthenon and the Erectheion, located?

- The Parliamentary Candidate
- The Greek Revolution

- Koine Greek
- Attic Greek

- Philosophy and history

- Isocrates

- From 6 to 15 years old

## Culture of Greece: Concept QA - test (50)

`Culture of Greece` / `QA_test`

**1.** The three main periods of Greek culture are ___

- Ancient Greece, Byzantine Greece, and Modern Greece
- Classical Greece, Hellenistic Greece, and Roman Greece
- Archaic Greece, Classical Greece, and Hellenistic Greece
- Minoan Greece, Mycenaean Greece, and Classical Greece

*Answer:* Ancient Greece, Byzantine Greece, and Modern Greece

*Original:* What are the three main periods of Greek culture?

**2.** On Crete, the palace at Knossos was built by the ancient civilization known as ___

- The Minoans
- The Mycenaeans
- The Cycladic people
- The Dorians

*Answer:* The Minoans

*Original:* Which ancient civilization on Crete built the palace at Knossos?

**3.** The famous temple located on the Acropolis of Athens is ___

- The Parthenon
- The Erectheion
- The Temple of Athena Nike
- The Propylaea

*Answer:* The Parthenon

*Original:* Which famous temple is located on the Acropolis of Athens?

**4.** Instead of marble, the building materials Byzantine architects primarily used were ___

- Stone and brick
- Wood and bronze
- Clay and limestone
- Concrete and steel

*Answer:* Stone and brick

*Original:* What building materials did Byzantine architects primarily use instead of marble?

**5.** The 19th-century architectural style widely used in modern Greece after independence was ___

- Neoclassical architecture
- Gothic Revival
- Art Nouveau
- Baroque

*Answer:* Neoclassical architecture

*Original:* Which 19th-century architectural style was widely used in modern Greece after independence?

**6.** The Athens Charter, a manifesto of the modernist movement, was signed and published in ___

- 1933
- 1925
- 1940
- 1928

*Answer:* 1933

*Original:* In what year was the Athens Charter, a manifesto of the modernist movement, signed and published?

**7.** The film considered the first Greek long movie is ___

- Golfo
- Stella
- The Drunkard
- Astero

*Answer:* Golfo

*Original:* What is considered the first Greek long movie?

**8.** According to ancient Greek belief, dancing was invented by ___

- The gods
- The Muses
- Orpheus
- Dionysus

*Answer:* The gods

*Original:* According to ancient Greek belief, who invented dancing?

**9.** The name of the important school of icon painting during Venetian rule in Greece is ___

- The Cretan School
- The Heptanese School
- The Macedonian School
- The Byzantine School

*Answer:* The Cretan School

# Golf

## Golf: Concept QA - validation (50)

`Golf` / `QA_train`

**1.** The primary objective in golf is ___

- To hit the ball into a series of holes in as few strokes as possible
- To hit the longest drive on every hole
- To avoid all bunkers and water
- To finish the round fastest

*Answer:* To hit the ball into a series of holes in as few strokes as possible

*Original:* What is the primary objective in golf?

**2.** A standard golf course typically has ___

- 18 holes
- 9 holes
- 12 holes
- 24 holes

*Answer:* 18 holes

*Original:* How many holes are typically found on a standard golf course?

**3.** The area where a golfer begins play on each hole is called the ___

- Teeing ground or tee box
- Fairway
- Rough
- Green

*Answer:* Teeing ground or tee box

*Original:* What is the area called where a golfer begins play on each hole?

**4.** The sections of terrain between the tee and the green are called ___

- Fairway, rough, and hazards
- Fairway and green only
- Bunkers and fringe
- Apron and collar

*Answer:* Fairway, rough, and hazards

*Original:* What are the sections of terrain between the tee and the green called?

**5.** In golf, a 'dogleg' is ___

- A hole that bends to the left or right
- A green with two tiers
- A fairway with many bunkers
- A water hazard near the tee

*Answer:* A hole that bends to the left or right

*Original:* What is a 'dogleg' in golf?

**6.** The modern game of golf originated in ___

- Scotland
- England
- Ireland
- United States

*Answer:* Scotland

*Original:* In which country did the modern game of golf originate?

**7.** The famous course in Scotland considered a pilgrimage site for golfers is ___

- The Old Course at St Andrews
- Carnoustie
- Royal Troon
- Muirfield

*Answer:* The Old Course at St Andrews

*Original:* What is the name of the famous course in Scotland considered a pilgrimage site for golfers?

**8.** In golf, stroke play is ___

- A format in which players try to complete the course in the fewest total strokes
- A format where each hole is worth one point
- A format where only drives count
- A scramble format with teams

*Answer:* A format in which players try to complete the course in the fewest total strokes

*Original:* What is stroke play in golf?

**9.** In golf, match play is ___

- A format where players compete hole-by-hole
- A format of total strokes over 18 holes
- A team format where best ball counts
- A format where only putts count

*Answer:* A format where players compete hole-by-hole

*Original:* What is match play in golf?

**10.** The small peg used on the tee box is called ___

- A tee
- A marker
- A divot tool
- A pegboard

*Answer:* A tee

*Original:* What is the small peg used on the tee box called?

**11.** The type of golf club primarily used for long-distance tee shots is the ___

- Driver
- Putter
- Wedge
- 7-iron

*Answer:* Driver

*Original:* Which type of golf club is primarily used for long-distance tee shots?

**12.** The type of golf club primarily used for short, controlled shots on the green is the ___

- Putter
- Driver
- Hybrid
- 3-wood

*Answer:* Putter

*Original:* Which type of golf club is primarily used for short, controlled shots on the green?

**13.** Most modern wood clubs are made from ___

- Metal or composite materials
- Solid wood
- Aluminum
- Ceramic

*Answer:* Metal or composite materials

*Original:* What material are most modern wood clubs made from?

**14.** Most modern irons are made from ___

- Steel alloy
- Aluminum
- Titanium foam
- Carbon fiber

*Answer:* Steel alloy

*Original:* What material are most modern irons made from?

**15.** During a round, a player is allowed to have in their bag a maximum of ___

- 14 clubs
- 10 clubs
- 12 clubs
- 16 clubs

*Answer:* 14 clubs

*Original:* How many clubs is a player allowed to have in their bag during a round?

**16.** In golf, 'par' represents ___

- The number of strokes a skilled golfer is expected to require on a hole
- Your personal best score
- The average of the group
- The maximum allowed strokes

*Answer:* The number of strokes a skilled golfer is expected to require on a hole

*Original:* What does 'par' represent in golf?

**17.** In golf, a par-3 hole is ___

- A hole where a skilled golfer is expected to complete play in 3 strokes
- A hole with three bunkers
- A hole completed in three putts
- A hole played three times

*Answer:* A hole where a skilled golfer is expected to complete play in 3 strokes

*Original:* What is a par-3 hole?

**18.** The term for the area with longer, thicker grass on a golf course is ___

- The rough
- The fairway
- The fringe
- The apron

*Answer:* The rough

**19.** The primary function of spikes on golf shoes is ___

- To increase traction
- To aerate the green
- To add weight
- For style only

*Answer:* To increase traction

**20.** The piece of equipment used to carry a player's clubs and accessories is the ___

- Golf bag
- Caddie cart
- Trolley
- Locker

*Answer:* Golf bag

**21.** A typical 9-hole round of golf takes ___

- Approximately two hours
- Thirty minutes
- Four hours
- Ninety minutes

*Answer:* Approximately two hours

**22.** In golf club construction, 'conforming' refers to ___

- Clubs built according to official rules and parameters
- Clubs fitted to your height
- Clubs made of steel
- Clubs approved by your coach

*Answer:* Clubs built according to official rules and parameters

*Answer:* The R&A and the USGA

**24.** In golf, a penalty stroke is ___

- An extra stroke added for a rule infraction or taking relief
- A free drop
- Losing the hole automatically
- Replaying the shot without penalty

*Answer:* An extra stroke added for a rule infraction or taking relief

*Original:* What is a penalty stroke in golf?

**25.** In golf, the penalty for a lost ball is ___

- One stroke plus a distance penalty
- Two strokes only
- Replay without penalty
- Loss of hole

*Answer:* One stroke plus a distance penalty

**26.** The purpose of golf etiquette is ___

- To ensure safety, fairness, and a good pace of play

- To allow coaching on the course

**27.** In golf, the role of a caddie is ___

- To carry a player's equipment and offer advice on play

- To mow the greens

**28.** The combined terrain features of a golf course between the tee and the green are known as ___

- The apron

- Tee markers
- Fairway posts
- Pin markers
- Boundary stakes

*Answer:* Tee markers

- The backswing
- The downswing

- To chip the ball high

*Answer:* To roll the ball into the cup

- A green with two tiers
- A par 5 with two lakes

**33.** The reason golf courses have varied terrain is ___

- Each course is unique in its layout and obstacles
- To meet a single global standard

**34.** The scoring format that compares the outcome of each individual hole between players is ___

- Match play

**35.** Under the rules of golf, when a player's ball moves unintentionally, the result is ___

- No penalty ever applies

**36.** One method a golfer uses to elevate the ball for the initial shot is ___

- Placing the ball on a tee

- Holding it in the air

- Use a club with higher loft

- Scratch golfer
- Bogey golfer

- To place the ball on a tee everywhere
- To improve your lie in the rough

- The area where the cup is located for putting

*Answer:* The area where the cup is located for putting

- 1860
- 1873
- 1895
- 1900

- Tee markers

## Golf: Concept QA - test (50)

`Golf` / `QA_test`

**1.** The sport that involves using clubs and a ball to hit it into a series of holes is ___

- Golf
- Croquet
- Polo
- Field hockey

*Answer:* Golf

*Original:* What sport involves using clubs and a ball to hit it into a series of holes?

**2.** The area on a golf course that contains the cup where the ball must be sunk is ___

- The putting green
- The fairway
- The fringe
- The tee box

*Answer:* The putting green

*Original:* Which area on a golf course contains the cup where the ball must be sunk?

**3.** Modern golf originated during ___

- The 15th century
- The 14th century
- The 16th century
- The 18th century

*Answer:* The 15th century

*Original:* During which century did modern golf originate?

**4.** The world's oldest major golf tournament is ___

- The Open Championship (British Open)
- The Masters Tournament
- The U.S. Open
- The PGA Championship

*Answer:* The Open Championship (British Open)

*Original:* What is the name of the world's oldest major golf tournament?

**5.** The Open Championship was first played at ___

- Prestwick Golf Club
- The Old Course at St Andrews
- Royal Troon
- Muirfield

*Answer:* Prestwick Golf Club

*Original:* At which golf club was The Open Championship first played?

**6.** In golf, the purpose of a tee is ___

- To elevate the ball for the first stroke on a hole
- To mark the ball’s position on the green
- To repair divots on the fairway
- To measure yardage to the hole

*Answer:* To elevate the ball for the first stroke on a hole

*Original:* What is the purpose of a tee in golf?

**7.** The club designed to hit the ball long distances from the tee is the ___

- Driver
- 3-wood
- 5-iron
- Putter

*Answer:* Driver

*Original:* Which club is designed to hit the ball long distances from the tee?

**8.** Hybrid clubs are designed to combine ___

- The features of woods and irons
- The features of wedges and putters
- The features of drivers and putters
- The properties of shafts and grips

*Answer:* The features of woods and irons

*Original:* What are hybrid clubs designed to combine?

**9.** Golf balls have dimples in order ___

- To reduce aerodynamic drag and increase distance
- To improve spin on the green only
- To reduce wear from clubface grooves
- To make the ball heavier in wind

*Answer:* To reduce aerodynamic drag and increase distance

*Original:* Why do golf balls have dimples?

**10.** In golf, the term 'green in regulation' (GIR) means ___

- Reaching the green in two strokes less than the hole’s par
- Reaching the green in one stroke less than par
- Reaching the green in two strokes over par
- Being on the green regardless of strokes taken

*Answer:* Reaching the green in two strokes less than the hole’s par

*Original:* What does the term 'green in regulation' (GIR) mean?

**11.** The typical overall par score for an 18-hole golf course is ___

- 70 to 72
- 60 to 62
- 66 to 68
- 74 to 76

*Answer:* 70 to 72

*Original:* What is the typical overall par score for an 18-hole golf course?

**12.** The term for hitting the ball into the hole with one stroke is ___

- Hole in one or ace
- Birdie
- Eagle
- Albatross

*Answer:* Hole in one or ace

*Original:* What is the term for hitting the ball into the hole with one stroke?

**13.** The term used for scoring one stroke under par on a hole is ___

- Birdie
- Par
- Bogey
- Eagle

*Answer:* Birdie

*Original:* What term is used for scoring one stroke under par on a hole?

**14.** The term used for scoring two strokes under par on a hole is ___

- Eagle
- Birdie
- Albatross
- Par

*Answer:* Eagle

*Original:* What term is used for scoring two strokes under par on a hole?

**15.** The term used for scoring one stroke over par on a hole is ___

- Bogey
- Par
- Double bogey
- Birdie

*Answer:* Bogey

*Original:* What term is used for scoring one stroke over par on a hole?

**16.** The term for a score of two strokes over par is ___

- Double bogey
- Bogey
- Triple bogey
- Double eagle

*Answer:* Double bogey

*Original:* What term describes a score of two strokes over par?

**17.** The name of the sand-filled hazard on a golf course is ___

- Bunker or sand trap
- The rough
- The fairway
- Water hazard

*Answer:* Bunker or sand trap

*Original:* What is the name of the sand-filled hazard on a golf course?

**18.** An 18-hole round of golf usually takes ___

- Approximately four hours
- About two hours
- Five to six hours
- About one hour and fifteen minutes

*Answer:* Approximately four hours

*Original:* How long does an 18-hole round of golf usually take?

**19.** Instead of walking the course, players often use ___

- A golf cart
- A pull/push trolley
- A caddie carrying the bag
- A bicycle

*Answer:* A golf cart

*Original:* What alternative do players often use instead of walking the course?

**20.** The underlying principle of the rules of golf is ___

- Fairness
- Leniency
- Speed of play above all
- Tradition only

*Answer:* Fairness

*Original:* What is the underlying principle of the rules of golf?

**21.** In golf, the penalty for hitting the wrong ball is ___

- Two strokes
- One stroke
- Loss of hole
- Replay the shot without penalty

*Answer:* Two strokes

*Original:* What is the penalty for hitting the wrong ball?

**22.** The main objective of a full golf swing is ___

- To hit the ball with maximum distance and accuracy
- To generate maximum backspin regardless of direction
- To keep the ball as low as possible every time
- To avoid any body rotation

*Answer:* To hit the ball with maximum distance and accuracy

*Original:* What is the main objective of a full golf swing?

**23.** The difference between a drive and an approach shot is that ___

- A drive is a long-distance tee shot; an approach shot is used to reach the green
- A drive is a putt from long range; an approach is any tee shot
- A drive is taken only with irons; an approach only with woods
- A drive is from bunkers; an approach is from the rough

*Answer:* A drive is a long-distance tee shot; an approach shot is used to reach the green

*Original:* What is the difference between a drive and an approach shot?

**24.** In golf, the purpose of a pitch shot is ___

- To deliver the ball onto the green from a short distance
- To hit long tee shots on par-5 holes
- To escape from deep rough with a low runner
- To roll the ball along the ground from the fringe

*Answer:* To deliver the ball onto the green from a short distance

*Original:* What is the purpose of a pitch shot in golf?

**25.** In golf, a chip shot is ___

- A short shot used to get the ball onto or near the green
- A long shot to reach the green from the fairway
- A high, soft bunker shot from the sand
- A tee shot on a par-3

*Answer:* A short shot used to get the ball onto or near the green

*Original:* What is a chip shot in golf?

**26.** A recreational golf group typically consists of ___

- Between 1 and 4 players
- Exactly 4 players only
- Between 2 and 6 players
- Between 3 and 5 players

*Answer:* Between 1 and 4 players

*Original:* How many players typically form a recreational golf group?

**27.** When a player's score on a hole is exactly the par value, it means that ___

- The player made par
- The player recorded their handicap
- The player improved their lie
- The player took a mulligan

*Answer:* The player made par

*Original:* What does it mean when a player's score on a hole is exactly the par value?

- Dogleg
- Fade
- Draw
- Swale

*Answer:* Dogleg

- Dimples
- Grooves
- Seams
- A matte finish coating

*Answer:* Dimples

- Penalty assessment

- Provisional ruling

*Answer:* Penalty assessment

*Original:* What term describes the process of adding extra strokes for rule infractions?

**31.** The scoring format that tallies every stroke taken over the course is ___

*Answer:* Stroke play

- Match play points

- Medal play

*Answer:* Stableford scoring system

**33.** In golf scoring, a double bogey signifies ___

- Two strokes over par
- One stroke over par
- Three strokes over par
- Two strokes under par

*Answer:* Two strokes over par

*Original:* What does a double bogey signify in golf scoring?

**34.** The main purpose of a handicap system in golf is ___

- To allow players of different abilities to compete fairly
- To select tee times for players
- To standardize equipment specifications
- To rank professional players globally

*Answer:* To allow players of different abilities to compete fairly

*Original:* What is the main purpose of a handicap system in golf?

- James II
- James I
- James III
- James V

*Answer:* James II

- James IV
- James III
- James V
- Charles I

*Answer:* James IV

**37.** Early Scottish golf courses were primarily laid out on ___

- Links land (coastal sand dunes)
- Parkland forests
- Mountain meadows
- Urban parks

*Answer:* Links land (coastal sand dunes)

*Original:* On what type of terrain were early Scottish golf courses primarily laid out?

**38.** Golfers often move their clubs using ___

- A golf trolley or carrying bag
- A motorized golf cart
- A caddie service
- A range basket

*Answer:* A golf trolley or carrying bag

*Original:* What do golfers often use to move their clubs if they choose not to walk?

**39.** One main advantage of the stroke play format is that ___

- Every stroke counts toward the total score
- Bad holes don’t matter because only holes won count
- It encourages skins-style gambling

*Answer:* Every stroke counts toward the total score

- One stroke over par
- One stroke under par
- Two strokes over par
- Three strokes over par

*Answer:* One stroke over par

**41.** The club specifically designed with a large, low-lofted head to maximize distance is the ___

- Driver
- 3-wood
- Hybrid
- Lob wedge

*Answer:* Driver

- Rolling the ball into the cup on the green
- Hitting from the tee on long holes
- Escaping from bunkers
- High-loft approach shots from 100 yards

*Answer:* Rolling the ball into the cup on the green

- Driver
- Sand wedge
- 9-iron
- Putter

*Answer:* Driver

- To match clubs to a player's physical attributes and swing
- To meet tournament regulations only
- To customize the shaft color
- To reduce the cost of clubs

*Answer:* To match clubs to a player's physical attributes and swing

- A putting style that involves anchoring the putter to the player's body
- Using a mallet-shaped putter head
- Gripping the putter with the palms only
- Putting while facing the hole (side-saddle)

*Answer:* A putting style that involves anchoring the putter to the player's body

- A conventional grip where the off-hand finger overlaps the dominant hand's finger

*Answer:* A conventional grip where the off-hand finger overlaps the dominant hand's finger

- Stroke play

- Scramble

- A shotgun start where all groups start on different holes

- Eagle

# Nazism

## Nazism: Concept QA - validation (50)

`Nazism` / `QA_train`

**1.** The doctrine the Nazis used to justify gaining lands for territorial expansion was ___

- Lebensraum
- Anschluss
- Gleichschaltung
- Autarky

*Answer:* Lebensraum

*Original:* What doctrine did the Nazis use to justify gaining lands for territorial expansion?

**2.** The 1922 event in Italy that Hitler admired and used as a model for the Beer Hall Putsch was ___

- March on Rome
- The Great Purge
- The October Revolution
- The Kapp Putsch

*Answer:* March on Rome

*Original:* What 1922 event in Italy did Hitler admire and use as a model for the Beer Hall Putsch?

**3.** According to Nazi pseudo-scientific theories, ethnic Germans were the master race known as the ___

- Nordic Aryans
- Anglo-Saxons
- Teutonic Knights
- Indo-Europeans

*Answer:* Nordic Aryans

*Original:* What master race did Nazi pseudo-scientific theories identify ethnic Germans as?

**4.** The 1926 publication called 'Der Nazi-Sozi' was published by ___

- Joseph Goebbels
- Heinrich Himmler
- Hermann Göring
- Gregor Strasser

*Answer:* Joseph Goebbels

*Original:* Who published the 1926 publication called "Der Nazi-Sozi"?

**5.** The group of people placed at the very bottom of the Nazi racial scale alongside Jews, Romanis, and blacks were ___

- Slavs
- Nordics
- Anglos
- Francos

*Answer:* Slavs

*Original:* What group of people were placed at the very bottom of the Nazi racial scale alongside Jews, Romanis, and blacks?

**6.** The girls' wing of the Nazi party was called the ___

- League of German Girls
- German Women's Front
- National Socialist Sisterhood
- Faith and Beauty Society

*Answer:* League of German Girls

*Original:* What was the name of the girls' wing of the Nazi party?

**7.** Hitler's belief that world peace required uncontested supremacy by the 'racially best' power was relayed to Walter Hewel by ___

- Rudolf Hess
- Martin Bormann
- Albert Speer
- Joachim von Ribbentrop

*Answer:* Rudolf Hess

*Original:* Who relayed to Walter Hewel Hitler's belief that world peace required uncontested supremacy by the "racially best" power?

**8.** Alongside the Enabling Act, the 1933 decree used to establish a one-party state was ___

- Reichstag Fire Decree
- Decree for the Protection of People and State
- The Nuremberg Laws
- The T4 Edict

*Answer:* Reichstag Fire Decree

*Original:* What 1933 decree, alongside the Enabling Act, was used to establish a one-party state?

**9.** The two major corporations among the largest contributors to the Nazi Party in early 1933 were ___

- IG Farben and Deutsche Bank
- Siemens and Krupp
- Volkswagen and BMW
- Thyssen and Allianz

*Answer:* IG Farben and Deutsche Bank

# Uranium

## Uranium: Concept QA - validation (50)

`Uranium` / `QA_train`

**1.** The chemical symbol for uranium is ___

- U
- Ur
- Un
- Rn

*Answer:* U

*Original:* What is the chemical symbol for uranium?

**2.** The atomic number of uranium is ___

- 92
- 90
- 91
- 94

*Answer:* 92

*Original:* What is the atomic number of uranium?

**3.** In the periodic table, uranium belongs to the ___

- Actinide series
- Lanthanide series
- Transition metals
- Alkali metals

*Answer:* Actinide series

*Original:* In which series of the periodic table is uranium classified?

**4.** The color of uranium metal is ___

- Silvery-grey
- Dull black
- Golden-yellow
- Bluish-silver

*Answer:* Silvery-grey

*Original:* What is the color of uranium metal?

**5.** The number of protons in a uranium atom is ___

- 92
- 90
- 91
- 93

*Answer:* 92

*Original:* How many protons does a uranium atom have?

**6.** Uranium was discovered in 1789 by ___

- Martin Heinrich Klaproth
- Antoine Lavoisier
- Dmitri Mendeleev
- Henri Becquerel

*Answer:* Martin Heinrich Klaproth

*Original:* Who discovered uranium in 1789?

**7.** On the question of whether uranium is denser than lead, the answer is ___

- Yes
- No
- Only when enriched
- Only at very low temperatures

*Answer:* Yes

*Original:* Is uranium denser than lead?

**8.** The acid that dissolves uranium is ___

- Hydrochloric acid
- Sulfuric acid
- Acetic acid
- Phosphoric acid

*Answer:* Hydrochloric acid

*Original:* Which acid dissolves uranium?

**9.** The property of uranium isotopes that makes them useful for dating the age of the Earth is ___

- Long half-life
- High electronegativity
- High conductivity
- Strong magnetism

*Answer:* Long half-life

*Original:* What property of uranium isotopes makes them useful for dating the age of the Earth?

**10.** Between 668 °C and 775 °C, the crystalline structure of uranium is ___

- Tetragonal (beta)
- Cubic (gamma)
- Orthorhombic (alpha)
- Hexagonal close-packed

*Answer:* Tetragonal (beta)

*Original:* What crystalline structure does uranium have between 668 °C and 775 °C?

**11.** In a reactor, the process that converts uranium-238 into plutonium-239 is ___

- Transmutation
- Fission
- Fusion
- Enrichment

*Answer:* Transmutation

*Original:* What process converts uranium-238 into plutonium-239 in a reactor?

**12.** Uranium that has had most of its U-235 removed is called ___

- Depleted uranium
- Natural uranium
- Low-enriched uranium
- Reprocessed uranium

*Answer:* Depleted uranium

*Original:* What is the term for uranium that has had most of its U-235 removed?

**13.** The half-life of uranium-235 is ___

- 704 million years
- 4.5 billion years
- 159,200 years
- 1.2 million years

*Answer:* 704 million years

*Original:* What is the half-life of uranium-235?

**14.** Uranium-233 is produced from ___

- Thorium
- Protactinium
- Neptunium
- Plutonium

*Answer:* Thorium

*Original:* From which element is uranium-233 produced?

**15.** Fission in uranium-235 is induced most effectively by ___

- Slow (thermal) neutrons
- Fast neutrons
- Epithermal neutrons
- Cold neutrons only

*Answer:* Slow (thermal) neutrons

*Original:* Which type of neutron induces fission in uranium-235 most effectively?

**16.** The chemical formula of the uranium compound used as nuclear reactor fuel is ___

- UO2
- UO₃
- UF₆
- U₃O₈

*Answer:* UO2

*Original:* What is the chemical formula of the uranium compound used as nuclear reactor fuel?

- UF₆
- UO2
- UCl₄
- U₃O₈

*Answer:* UF₆

*Original:* What chemical compound of uranium is used in the enrichment process?

- Gaseous diffusion process
- Gas centrifuge
- Laser isotope separation
- Plasma separation

*Answer:* Gaseous diffusion process

- 1942
- 1938
- 1945
- 1952

*Answer:* 1942

*Answer:* Little Boy

- 3 to 5

*Answer:* 6 to 8

- Gamma radiation

- Enrico Fermi

- World War II

# Valentine's Day

## Valentine's Day: Concept QA - validation (50)

`Valentine's Day` / `QA_train`

**1.** Many parts of the Eastern Orthodox Church celebrate Saint Valentine's Day in honor of the Roman presbyter Saint Valentine on ___

- February 14
- July 6
- July 30
- January 5

*Answer:* July 6

*Original:* On what date do many parts of the Eastern Orthodox Church celebrate Saint Valentine's Day in honor of Roman presbyter Saint Valentine?

**2.** The Japanese term for chocolate given to a loved one is ___

- Giri-choko
- Tomo-choko
- Honmei-choko
- Jibun-choko

*Answer:* Honmei-choko

*Original:* What is the Japanese term for chocolate given to a loved one?

**3.** Esther Howland lived in the city of ___

- Boston, Massachusetts
- Worcester, Massachusetts
- Providence, Rhode Island
- Springfield, Illinois

*Answer:* Worcester, Massachusetts

*Original:* In what city did Esther Howland live?

**4.** The day celebrated in South Korea on November 11 is ___

- White Day
- Pepero Day
- Black Day
- Rose Day

*Answer:* Pepero Day

*Original:* What day is celebrated in South Korea on November 11?

**5.** The company that introduced Valentine's Day to Japan in 1936 was ___

- Meiji Co.
- Lotte Corp
- Morozoff Ltd.
- Ezaki Glico

*Answer:* Morozoff Ltd.

*Original:* What company introduced Valentine's Day to Japan in 1936?

- Mozaffar ad-Din Shah Qajar

- Fath-Ali Shah Qajar
- Ahmad Shah Qajar

*Answer:* Naser al-Din Shah Qajar

- The Valentine Mill

- The Love Press
- Heart Factory

*Answer:* Cupid's Manufactory

*Original:* What term did Charles Dickens use to describe the mass production of Valentines?

**8.** In the 14th and 15th centuries, the day came to be associated with romantic love through the flourishing notions of ___

- Platonic love
- Courtly love

- Divine love

*Answer:* Courtly love

**9.** The department store that ran a Valentine sale in Japan in 1958 was ___

- Mitsukoshi
- Takashimaya
- Isetan

*Answer:* Isetan

*Original:* What department store ran a Valentine sale in Japan in 1958?

**10.** On Tu B'Av, girls would traditionally wear ___

- Red dresses

- Blue dresses
- Pink dresses

*Answer:* White dresses

- Nero
- Claudius II
- Caligula
- Marcus Aurelius

*Answer:* Claudius II

**12.** Valentine of Rome was martyred in the year ___

- 269
- 313
- 496
- 144

*Answer:* 269

*Original:* In what year was Valentine of Rome martyred?

- Athens
- Rome

- Florence

*Answer:* Rome

**14.** The Finnish name for Valentine's Day, meaning 'Friend's Day', is ___

- Ystävänpäivä
- Sõbrapäev
- Vennskapsdag
- Vänskapens dag

*Answer:* Ystävänpäivä

*Original:* What is the Finnish name for Valentine's Day, meaning 'Friend's Day'?

- Blue Day
- White Day

- Sweet Day

- February 14
